# RESEARCH TASK (read-only) — scope an error-handling round in `dev_sgi_re.c`

## READ THIS FIRST — PLEASE DO NOT SIMPLY RATIFY

**Check the premises, not just the derivations.** Where I state something as fact, ask whether it is
true. A seat that re-derives a premise from something this task did NOT give it is worth more than
one that agrees.

**THE TREE UNDER REVIEW IS `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`.** There are three pristine-2021-upstream copies of
GXemul under the project root (`./src/` and two under `_archive/audit-results-*/builds/`). They are
NOT this fork. If your line numbers disagree with mine by hundreds you are reading one of them.

**Where this task states an ARITHMETIC claim — a count, a set of offsets — ENUMERATE it rather than
reasoning about it.**

**VERDICT LINE FIRST**, so your answer survives truncation.

---

## Context

GXemul is a machine emulator. `dev_sgi_re.c` models the SGI CRIME rendering engine. This fork is a
maintenance effort whose standing goal is **robust error handling**: where the model meets a
register value or access width it does not implement, it should report the condition and continue,
rather than calling `exit(1)` and ending the emulator process. Ending the process on unimplemented
input is poor error handling in a device model — a simulated peripheral should degrade gracefully.

Several rounds have already converted such sites in this file (`#497`, `#499`, `#500`, `#504`). This
task scopes the remaining ones **before** a design proposal is written. Nothing is being changed.

## 1. THE COUNT — please re-derive it

The queue entry for this work has carried three different counts and I corrected it twice:

    entry title said           21
    my first correction said   19      (it counted commented-out lines)
    the derived live count     12      grep -cE '^[[:space:]]+exit\(1\);'   vs 19 raw matches

**Q1: re-derive the live count by a method of your choosing and say what you counted.** If you get
something other than 12 I want the method, not just the number — two agreeing greps already misled
me on this file because both counted comment text.

## 2. AN ORDERING CONSTRAINT — the interesting part of this round

`regress/sgi_re_width_probe.py` is a regression test wired into `gate_arm.sh`. It ships **six
`<site> ld kill` rows** whose expectation records CURRENT behaviour — that an 8-byte read at each of
six register windows still ends the process:

    row(nm + " ld kill", how + ("+txt" if ok_txt else "-txt"), "exit1+txt",
        "len=8 READ STILL ENDS THE PROCESS here -- untouched")

So **improving the error handling turns a passing test suite red by succeeding**, six rows' worth.
This project's rule for that case is an ordering discipline: re-pin the six expectations FIRST,
demonstrate the COMMITTED build failing under the new expectations, and only THEN change the model.
Otherwise re-pinning a test cannot be distinguished from fitting the test to the code.

## 3. THE EXPECTATION DESIGN I HAVE PRE-REGISTERED — please attack it

I did not invent this. `#499` re-pinned a neighbouring row **in this same test file** under the same
circumstances, and its comment records that **two obvious expectations were measured USELESS before
a third was adopted**:

* **Termination-only** (`how == "exit1"`) — records THAT the process ended, never WHICH site ended
  it. A message change that left the `exit(1)` in place scored **35/35** against a matched control at
  34/35.
* **`exit0`** — exit status cannot separate *handled the value, then quit* from *ended with status
  0*. A variant scored GREEN on an `exit0+txt` expectation while the process was not alive.

What survived records **three** independent observations:

* `how` — the process is still running;
* `txt` — **one contiguous literal** including the address and data fields. Two separate substring
  tests are weaker: `-- declined.` is emitted by six other sites in this file and by a second device,
  so a bare substring cross-matches;
* `v0` — **the handled read reaches the function's common tail and returns a defined 0.** `v0` is
  preset to a sentinel by the test's `load()` helper, so *returned 0* and *never reached the tail*
  are distinguishable. This is the observation that catches a `return 0` (which `memory_rw` converts
  to −1, raising a bus-error exception) and an `exit(0)` confined to the read branch — **neither of
  which a write-side expectation can observe.** These six sites are read paths, so it is not
  optional here.

**Q2: is that the right expectation set for these six, or does a read path need an observation the
earlier site did not?** Name any channel the three above cannot see.

**Q3: what is the smallest change to the model that would restore the old behaviour at one of these
sites while still satisfying a test built that way?** Name the row you expect to stay green. This is
a test-quality question: a regression test that cannot notice the behaviour returning is not doing
its job.

## 4. THE DISPOSITION QUESTION

When a device model meets input it does not implement, this project recognises seven dispositions:
**1 remove the check entirely · 2 ignore the access silently · 3 ignore it and report once ·
4 accept it (the model already computes the right answer) · 5 signal a bus error to the guest ·
6 accept it into invented storage · 7 implement the missing behaviour.**

**Q4: which disposition does each live site want, and do they all want the SAME one?** A round here
may cover a second site only if one design covers both and one test covers both with no new
observation — so if these twelve split across dispositions, this is several rounds and I would
rather know now.

**SCOPE YOUR READING TO `GXEMUL-SEC/src/` AND `GXEMUL-SEC/regress/*.py`.**  Do not read
`GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/` or `GXEMUL-SEC/tools/pipeline/ledger.json`: they are this project's
archive of past measurements, they are large, and nothing in them is needed for the questions below.
Everything you need is in the model source and the test file.

**Before answering, GREP THE SOURCE FILE AND ITS NEIGHBOURS FOR THE EXISTING IDIOM.** The tree's own
precedents are part of the design space, and this project has twice found whole dispositions that
way. In particular, `#497`, `#499`, `#500` and `#504` each converted a site in this same file, and
their choices constrain what is consistent.

**And check disposition 5 is even AVAILABLE here before recommending it.** Signalling a bus error
works because `memory_rw.c` dispatches an exception on a failed device access — for MIPS and M88K. A
disposition the target CPU cannot deliver is not an option, however good it looks.

## 5. WHAT I AM NOT ASKING

Do not write the change. Do not write test rows. I want: the count, the classification, whether this
is one round or several, and whether the pre-registered expectation set survives contact.

**If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE — say so.** The last two rounds' best
findings arrived that way, including two defects in code everyone had called correct.

    1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
    2: #
    3: #  #497 widthkillclass -- the detector.
    4: #
    5: #  CONTRACT (vacuity-floor clause (e)) -- which edits this detector is designed
    6: #  to catch, and which it is NOT:
    7: #
    8: #    CATCHES
    9: #      * removal or reinstatement of ANY of the six width kills, at any one of
   10: #        the six sites individually (the `sw surv' rows are per site);
   11: #      * narrowing the decline so it applies to writes only, i.e.
   12: #        `len != 8' -> `len != 8 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE'  (the `lw surv' rows);
   13: #      * ANY decline that does not reach the function's common tail -- an early
   14: #        `return', or `return 0' (which memory_rw coerces to -1 and turns into a
   15: #        guest EXCEPTION_DBE).  The `lw surv' rows check the VALUE the declined
   16: #        read returns, not merely that the host survived: the destination
   17: #        register is preset to a sentinel, so "tail ran, wrote 0" and "load
   18: #        never completed" are distinguishable.  A survival-only oracle at a
   19: #        refusal-effect site is vacuous by construction, and this row was one
   20: #        until the d_returnzero mutant was built;
   21: #      * deleting the latch, or dropping the `d->warned |= BIT' set, so the
   22: #        message floods  (the `sw lat1' rows COUNT; they do not test presence);
   23: #      * making two or more sites share a latch bit  (`xsite 6msg');
   24: #      * mis-attributing a window's message to another window (`xsite order');
   25: #      * growth of the width predicate that swallows LEGAL 8-byte accesses
   26: #        (the `accept' rows, probed at an offset with bit 3 SET so that a
   27: #        `|| (relative_addr & 8)' tightening reddens them);
   28: #      * silent removal of the six READ kills or of the final catch-all, which
   29: #        this round deliberately does NOT touch  (`<s> ld kill' x6, `other kill').
   30: #
   31: #    *** AND IT PINS THE ROUND'S CENTRAL DISCLOSURE, RATHER THAN ASSERTING IT.
   32: #        The width guard is evaluated FIRST, so after #497 a 4-byte read
   33: #        DECLINES -- but an ordinary 8-byte `ld' to each of the six windows
   34: #        STILL ENDS THE HOST, because len == 8 passes the width guard and falls
   35: #        into the read arm's exit(1) (dev_sgi_re.c lines 245 262 279 296 346
   36: #        362).  Fixing six width kills leaves EXACTLY six read kills at the same
   37: #        six offsets.  The `<s> ld kill' rows measure that at every window, so a
   38: #        future reader cannot conclude from `lw surv' that reads were made safe.
   39: #        Filed as sgirekills.  *** ITS WITNESS IS THIS FILE: the six `<site> ld kill`
   40: #        rows below ARE rung-3 symptom demonstrations of that defect, and the ledger's
   41: #        witness field names them.  This comment used to say the row `has no witness
   42: #        yet' -- written before those rows existed and left standing after they shipped,
   43: #        so the file denied being the witness it had become.  Found by the codex seat. ***
   44: #
   45: #    DOES NOT CATCH
   46: #      * anything in DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_de) or (sgi_mte), including sgi_de's own
   47: #        width kill (accept set {4,8}, filed sgidewidth) and sgi_mte's
   48: #        unreachable one (filed sgimtewidth);
   49: #      * the LINEAR out-of-bounds index itself.  Observing re_linear_b[0] being
   50: #        clobbered needs a READ path, and every read arm here still exit(1)s.
   51: #        The aliasing is pinned by offsetof in PRETABLE.out section 1.2, not by
   52: #        a row.  Owed to sgirekills.
   53: #
   54: #    ONE THING THE CONTRACT UNDERSTATED, corrected before shipping rather than
   55: #    left as a modest-sounding false claim: an earlier draft said there is NO
   56: #    anchored cell-position row.  There is.  Each `accept' row matches the arm's
   57: #    own debug() text INCLUDING THE INDEX AND THE VALUE -- e.g.
   58: #    "d->re_tlb_a[4] = 0x1122" -- so it is anchored to a SPECIFIC cell by a
   59: #    channel outside the decode under test, which is clause (f)'s requirement.
   60: #    A re-map of the index arithmetic (>> 1 -> >> 2) or of the byte packing
   61: #    reddens it.  Window-level translations are additionally caught by the
   62: #    per-site attribution rows, since each checks the message naming ITS OWN
   63: #    symbol.  What remains genuinely uncovered is a re-map that preserves both
   64: #    index and symbol.
   65: #
   66: import os, pty, re, select, sys, time
   67: 
   68: BIN   = sys.argv[1]
   69: LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
   70: DUMMY = "/tmp/dummy497.bin"

   95: def decl(sym):
   96:     """The literal a WIDTH decline emits.  Deliberately includes ' -- declined.'
   97:     so it cannot also match the read kill's 'TODO: read from <sym>'."""
   98:     return "for " + sym + " -- declined."
   99: 
  100: OP_NOP      = 0x00000000
  101: OP_B_SELF   = 0x1000ffff
  102: OP_SW_T0_A3 = 0xace80000          # sw   $t0, 0($a3)
  103: OP_SD_T0_A3 = 0xfce80000          # sd   $t0, 0($a3)
  104: OP_LW_V0_AT = 0x8c220000          # lw   $v0, 0($at)
  105: OP_LD_V0_AT = 0xdc220000          # ld   $v0, 0($at)
  106: 
  107: rows = []
  108: def row(name, got, want, note=""):
  109:     ok = (got == want)
  110:     rows.append((name, ok))
  111:     print("%-18s %-12s %-12s %-46s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note,
  112:                                           "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
  113:     return ok
  114: 
  115: def session(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.2):
  116:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
  117:     if pid == 0:
  118:         os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "sgi", "-e", "ip32", "-M", "64",
  119:                        "0x80001000:" + DUMMY])
  120:         os._exit(127)
  121:     buf = ""
  122:     def rd(t):
  123:         nonlocal buf
  124:         end = time.time() + t
  125:         while time.time() < end:
  126:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
  127:             if not r:
  128:                 continue
  129:             try:
  130:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
  131:                 if not d:
  132:                     return False
  133:                 buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
  134:             except OSError:
  135:                 return False
  136:         return True
  137:     alive = rd(settle)
  138:     for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
  139:         try:
  140:             os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
  141:         except OSError:
  142:             alive = False
  143:             break
  144:         if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.25):
  145:             alive = False
  146:             break
  147:     try:
  148:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.4)
  149:     except OSError:
  150:         pass
  151:     try:
  152:         os.close(fd)
  153:     except OSError:
  154:         pass
  155:     how = "nostatus"
  156:     try:
  157:         _, stat = os.waitpid(pid, 0)
  158:         if os.WIFSIGNALED(stat):
  159:             how = "sig%d" % os.WTERMSIG(stat)
  160:         elif os.WIFEXITED(stat):
  161:             how = "exit%d" % os.WEXITSTATUS(stat)
  162:     except OSError:
  163:         pass
  164:     return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive, how
  165: 
  166: def printed(t, reg):
  167:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
  168:     if not m:
  169:         return "absent"
  170:     #  Normalise width: a 64-bit register may print sign/zero-extended, and a
  171:     #  row comparing raw strings would then fail on formatting rather than on
  172:     #  behaviour.
  173:     return "0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16)
  174: 
  175: class Addr(object):
  176:     def __init__(self, base): self.a = base
  177:     def take(self, n):
  178:         got = self.a; self.a += 4 * (n + 2) + 0x20; return got
  179: 
  180: def run_ops(ops, at):
  181:     put = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(ops)]
  182:     put += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops), OP_B_SELF),
  183:             "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops) + 4, OP_NOP)]
  184:     return put + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(ops)]
  185: 
  186: def store(A, addr, op=OP_SW_T0_A3, val="0x12345678"):
  187:     return ["a3=0x%x" % addr, "t0=%s" % val] + run_ops([op], A.take(1))
  188: 
  189: #  THE DESTINATION REGISTER IS PRESET TO A SENTINEL, NOT TO 0.
  190: #  Preset to 0 the row cannot tell "the common tail ran and wrote odata (0)"
  191: #  from "the load never completed at all" -- both leave v0 == 0.  With a
  192: #  sentinel the two separate: a declined read that reaches the tail yields 0,
  193: #  and a read that faulted or never landed yields the sentinel.
  194: SENTINEL = 0xdeadbeef
  195: 
  196: def load(A, addr, op=OP_LW_V0_AT):
  197:     return ["at=0x%x" % addr, "v0=0x%x" % SENTINEL] + run_ops([op], A.take(1))

  217: # ------------------------------------------------ per site x 6

  221:     # 1. a len=4 WRITE must be DECLINED, not fatal
  222:     A = Addr(CODE)

  224:     row(nm + " sw surv", how, "exit0", "len=4 WRITE declined, host survives")
  225: 
  226:     # 2. the latch: TWO declined accesses in ONE session -> exactly ONE message
  227:     A = Addr(CODE)
  228:     t, alive, how = session(store(A, base) + store(A, base + 4))
  229:     row(nm + " sw lat1", t.count(lit), 1,
  230:         "(once): COUNT over 2 accesses -- presence would pass unlatched")
  231: 
  232:     # 3. a len=4 READ must ALSO be declined.
  233:     #    NOTE: the width arm is evaluated BEFORE the read arm, so a len=4 read
  234:     #    takes the (now declining) width arm and never reaches the still-live
  235:     #    read kill.  An 8-byte read still dies -- see `ld readkill' below.
  236:     #    This row is what kills M1 (`len != 8 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE').
  237:     A = Addr(CODE)
  238:     t, alive, how = session(load(A, base) + ["print v0"])
  239:     got = how + "/" + (printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died")
  240:     row(nm + " lw surv", got, "exit0/0x0",
  241:         "declined READ reaches the tail and returns a DEFINED 0")
  242: 
  243:     # 4. ACCEPT side (de-escalation clause).  Offset +8 has bit 3 SET on
  244:     #    purpose: a `|| (relative_addr & 8)' growth must redden this.
  245:     A = Addr(CODE)
  246:     t, alive, how = session(store(A, base + 8, OP_SD_T0_A3, ACC_VAL))
  247:     row(nm + " accept", "1" if acctext in t else "0", "1",
  248:         "LEGAL len=8 write still lands (probed at +8, bit 3 set)")
  249:

  256: present = [decl(sym) for _n, _b, sym, _a in SITES]
  257: n_present = sum(1 for lit in present if t.count(lit) == 1)
  258: row("xsite 6msg", n_present, 6,
  259:     "six windows, ONE session: 6 distinct latches (kills bit collision)")
  260: 
  261: pos = [t.find(lit) for lit in present]
  262: ordered = all(p >= 0 for p in pos) and pos == sorted(pos)
  263: row("xsite order", "1" if ordered else "0", "1",
  264:     "ANCHOR: message order == the order the guest issued them")
  265: 
  266: # ------------------------------------------------ contract / non-regression
  267: #  THE LEFTOVER, MEASURED AT EVERY WINDOW rather than argued from the pairing:
  268: #  len == 8 passes the width guard and reaches the still-live read kill.
  269: for nm, base, sym, _a in SITES:
  270:     A = Addr(CODE)
  271:     t, alive, how = session(load(A, base, OP_LD_V0_AT))
  272:     ok_txt = ("TODO: read from " + sym) in t
  273:     row(nm + " ld kill", how + ("+txt" if ok_txt else "-txt"), "exit1+txt",
  274:         "len=8 READ STILL KILLS THE HOST here -- untouched (sgirekills)")
  275: 
  276: A = Addr(CODE)
  277: #  *** RE-PINNED BY #499, WHICH REMOVED THIS KILL.  Three channels replace one, and
  278: #  BOTH of the obvious oracles were measured VACUOUS before this one was chosen. ***
  279: #
  280: #  THE OLD ROW was `row("other kill", how, "exit1")' -- DEATH-ONLY.  It observed THAT
  281: #  the catch-all killed, never WHICH kill it was, so a change to the message that left
  282: #  exit(1) standing passed: MEASURED 35/35 against a matched control at 34/35.  Filed
  283: #  as `otherkilldeath', and its own note prescribed the repair used here.
  284: #
  285: #  THE OBVIOUS RE-PIN, `how == "exit0"', IS WORSE, and this is the part that had to be
  286: #  built rather than argued.  A one-token reinstatement of the death as exit(0) prints
  287: #  the announce and then dies; exit status CANNOT distinguish "declined, then quit"
  288: #  from "died with status 0".  MEASURED, variant `mexit0': the exit0+txt oracle scores
  289: #  GREEN (how=exit0, txt=True) while alive=False.  Filed as `exit0oracle'.
  290: #
  291: #  SO THE ROW PINS THREE THINGS:
  292: #    how    the host survives the declined WRITE;
  293: #    txt    ONE CONTIGUOUS literal, address and data field included.  Two independent
  294: #           substring tests are a weaker claim: "-- declined." is emitted by the six
  295: #           #497 width declines and by #498's sgi_de decline as well, and this probe's
  296: #           own decl() helper exists because a bare substring cross-matches;
  297: #    v0     a declined READ REACHES THE COMMON TAIL and lands a DEFINED 0.  v0 is
  298: #           preset to SENTINEL by load(), so "wrote odata (0)" and "never landed" are
  299: #           distinguishable -- the same reason the `lw surv' rows carry it.  This is
  300: #           what kills `mret0' (return 0 -> memory_rw.c coerces to -1 -> EXCEPTION_DBE)
  301: #           and `mrdexit0' (exit(0) in the READ branch only), neither of which any
  302: #           write-only oracle can see.
  303: #
  304: #  ORDER PROOF: the new oracle was measured RED on the COMMITTED pre-#499 build BEFORE
  305: #  the code changed -- table500b.out, variant `base', got=exit1/notxt/died against
  306: #  want=exit0/txt/0x0.  And it is not fitted to the fix either: it was PRE-REGISTERED
  307: #  by #497 in commit 112867a (2026-08-30 22:41), six hours before #499's baseline
  308: #  3ded1e8, in the very note this comment replaces.
  309: #
  310: #  CONTRACT -- so a later escape is a breach or an exclusion, not an argument.  This
  311: #  row covers the catch-all's WRITE announce and the READ tail-return.  It does NOT
  312: #  pin the LATCH (one access per direction here, so first == only) nor the READ
  313: #  announce text.  Those live in sgi_re_unmapped_probe.py (`wr lat1', `rd lat1',
  314: #  `rd decl'), WHICH MUST BE GATE-WIRED -- unwired, that coverage exists nowhere. ***
  315: DECL_W = ("unimplemented write to address 0x1800, "
  316:           "data=0x0000000012345678 -- declined.  (once) ]")
  317: t, alive, how = session(store(A, OUTSIDE) + load(A, OUTSIDE) + ["print v0"])
  318: got = "%s/%s/%s" % (how, "txt" if t.count(DECL_W) == 1 else "notxt",
  319:                    printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died")
  320: row("other decl", got, "exit0/txt/0x0",
  321:     "#499: declines, announces once, declined read lands a defined 0")
  322: 
  323: ok = sum(1 for _, o in rows if o)
  324: print("")
  325: print("OPUS497T_RESULT=%d/%d" % (ok, len(rows)))
  326: print("OPUS497T_LABEL=%s" % LABEL)
  327: print("OPUS497T_DONE")

   57: 
   58: /*
   59:  *  #497: one latch bit per WIDTH-guard site in DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_re).
   60:  *
   61:  *  SIX FLAGS, NOT ONE.  dev_wdc.c's own comment adjudicates this: sharing a
   62:  *  latch is licensed only across sites that are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE at
   63:  *  runtime.  These six are six different address windows, and any driver
   64:  *  bringing up the rendering engine walks all of them in ONE session, so a
   65:  *  shared latch would report the first odd-width access and silence the
   66:  *  other five.
   67:  */
   68: enum {
   69: 	SGIRE_W_TLBA_LEN  = 1 << 0,
   70: 	SGIRE_W_TLBB_LEN  = 1 << 1,
   71: 	SGIRE_W_TLBC_LEN  = 1 << 2,
   72: 	SGIRE_W_TEX_LEN   = 1 << 3,
   73: 	SGIRE_W_LINA_LEN  = 1 << 4,
   74: 	SGIRE_W_LINB_LEN  = 1 << 5,
   75: 
   76: 	/*  #498: sgi_de's width decline.  ONE bit, not six: the six above are one
   77: 	    per SITE because six windows with six distinct message texts are walked
   78: 	    in a single bring-up session, and one latch would report the first and
   79: 	    silence five.  sgi_de has ONE guard and ONE message text, so the house
   80: 	    per-message-site rule gives one bit.  Measured cost: `sb' then `sh' at
   81: 	    one offset announces len=1 and never len=2 -- accepted, and small,
   82: 	    because the message already prints the width.
   83: 
   84: 	    It must be its OWN bit.  Reusing SGIRE_W_TLBA_LEN scored 23/25: the
   85: 	    four handlers share one malloc'd struct, so any odd-width access to
   86: 	    sgi_re's TLB_A window would silence sgi_de's decline entirely.  */
   87: 	SGIRE_W_DE_LEN    = 1 << 6,
   88: 
   89: 	/*  #499: the unmatched-address catch-all.  TWO bits, one per DIRECTION:
   90: 	    a driver probing an unmapped window commonly reads and writes it in
   91: 	    one pass, and one bit would announce the first and silence the other.
   92: 
   93: 	    PLACED AFTER SGIRE_W_DE_LEN ON PURPOSE.  An earlier draft inserted
   94: 	    these two after SGIRE_W_LINB_LEN (1 << 5), which left the enum ending
   95: 	    at 1 << 6 -- so a maintainer scanning to the LAST enumerator for the
   96: 	    next free bit would take 1 << 7, WHICH THESE TWO LINES BELOW ALREADY
   97: 	    USE.  A duplicate enumerator is legal C and silent, and all four
   98: 	    handlers share one malloc'd struct, so the collision crosses devices.
   99: 
  100: 	    (Wording sharpened after a pass-2 seat read "which is already used"
  101: 	    as a standalone claim about 1 << 7 and reported the comment as
  102: 	    self-contradictory against the code three lines down.  The claim was
  103: 	    right and the sentence did not say BY WHAT.)  */
  104: 	SGIRE_W_UNMAP_WR  = 1 << 7,
  105: 	SGIRE_W_UNMAP_RD  = 1 << 8,
  106: 
  107: 	/*  #500: draw_primitive()'s unimplemented drawing op.  ITS OWN BIT,
  108: 	    for the reason #498 measured on SGIRE_W_DE_LEN: a latch shared with
  109: 	    another site is silenced by that site's ordinary traffic, so the
  110: 	    announce this fix exists to make would never fire.
  111: 
  112: 	    (This comment used to end "The next free bit is 1 << 10; the enum
  113: 	    ends HERE on purpose so that a maintainer scanning to the last
  114: 	    enumerator finds it."  BOTH HALVES WENT FALSE when sgirandompixel
  115: 	    added SGIRE_W_BADMODE = 1 << 10 BELOW this line.  Demoted rather
  116: 	    than deleted, because the REASONING is still why the convention
  117: 	    exists -- and leaving it live would have pointed the next reader at
  118: 	    a taken bit, which is the duplicate-enumerator collision #499
  119: 	    already paid for and warns about four lines up.  The live
  120: 	    next-free claim is on the LAST enumerator, where it belongs.)  */
  121: 	SGIRE_W_PRIM_OP   = 1 << 9,
  122: 
  123: 	/*  sgirandompixel: horrible_getputpixel()'s unimplemented-mode arm.  ITS
  124: 	    OWN BIT, for the reason #498 measured and #500 repeated -- the four
  125: 	    handlers share one malloc'd struct, so a latch shared with another
  126: 	    site is silenced by that site's ordinary traffic and the announce
  127: 	    never fires.  This one matters more than most: the helper is
  128: 	    PER-PIXEL, called inside line, rectangle and tiled-MTE loops, so an
  129: 	    UNLATCHED announce is a per-pixel flood rather than a diagnostic.
  130: 	    The next free bit is 1 << 11; the enum ends HERE on purpose, as
  131: 	    above.  */
  132: 	SGIRE_W_BADMODE   = 1 << 10,
  133: };
  134: 
  135: struct sgi_re_data {
  136: 	// Rendering engine registers:
  137: 	uint16_t	re_tlb_a[256];
  138: 	uint16_t	re_tlb_b[256];
  139: 	uint16_t	re_tlb_c[256];
  140: 	uint16_t	re_tex[112];
  141: 	// todo: clip_ids registers.
  142: 	uint32_t	re_linear_a[32];
  143: 	uint32_t	re_linear_b[32];
  144: 
  145: 	// Drawing engine registers:
  146: 	uint32_t	de_reg[DEV_SGI_DE_LENGTH / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  147: 
  148: 	// Memory transfer engine registers:
  149: 	uint32_t	mte_reg[DEV_SGI_MTE_LENGTH / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  150: 
  151: 	/*
  152: 	 *  #497: width-guard decline latches; see the enum above.
  153: 	 *
  154: 	 *  PLACED LAST, DELIBERATELY, AND THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING.
  155: 	 *  re_linear_a/b are written at [tlbi] AND [tlbi+1] with tlbi able to
  156: 	 *  reach 31, so index 32 of either array lands on whichever field
  157: 	 *  FOLLOWS it.  An earlier draft of this change put `warned' between
  158: 	 *  re_linear_b and de_reg; measured with offsetof, that made
  159: 	 *  re_linear_b[32] alias `warned' rather than de_reg[0] -- which both
  160: 	 *  falsified the adjacency the two LINEAR site comments cite, and made
  161: 	 *  an over-run SET WARNING LATCH BITS and silence this device's own
  162: 	 *  diagnostics.  Keeping the latch after mte_reg restores the
  163: 	 *  documented adjacency: re_linear_a[32] is re_linear_b[0] and
  164: 	 *  re_linear_b[32] is de_reg[0].
  165: 	 */
  166: 	uint32_t	warned;
  167: };
  168: 
  169: 
  170: /*
  171:  *  horrible_getputpixel():
  172:  *
  173:  *  This routine gets/puts a pixel in one of the tiles, from the perspective of
  174:  *  the rendering/drawing engine. Given x and y, it figures out which tile
  175:  *  number it is, and then finally does a slow read/write to get/put the pixel
  176:  *  at the correct sub-coordinates within the tile.
  177:  *
  178:  *  Tiles are always 512 _bytes_ wide, and 128 pixels high. For 32-bit color
  179:  *  modes, for example, that means 128 x 128 pixels.
  180:  *
  181:  *  For "linear" modes, y is ignored and x is an offset to select which linear
  182:  *  TLB entry to use.
  183:  */
  184: void horrible_getputpixel(int put, struct cpu* cpu, struct sgi_re_data* d,
  185: 	int x, int y, uint32_t* color, int mode)
  186: {
  187: 	uint32_t color_mode = mode & DE_MODE_TYPE_MASK;
  188: 	int bufdepth = 1 << ((mode >> 8) & 3); 
  189: 
  190: 	// dst_mode (see NetBSD's crmfbreg.h):
  191: 	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_A           0x00000000
  192: 	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_B           0x00000400
  193: 	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_C           0x00000800
  194: 	// #define DE_MODE_LIN_A           0x00001000
  195: 	// #define DE_MODE_LIN_B           0x00001400
  196: 	uint32_t tlb_mode = (mode >> 10) & 0x7;
  197: 	int linear = tlb_mode > 3;
  198: 
  199: 	if (!linear && (x < 0 || y < 0 || x >= 2048 || y >= 2048))
  200: 		return;
  201: 
  202: 	int tilewidth_in_pixels = 512 / bufdepth;
  203: 	
  204: 	int tile_nr_x = x / tilewidth_in_pixels;
  205: 	int tile_nr_y = y >> 7;
  206: 
  207: 	unsigned int tile_nr = tile_nr_y * 16 + tile_nr_x;
  208: 
  209: 	y &= 127;
  210: 	int xofs = (x % tilewidth_in_pixels) * bufdepth;
  211: 	int ofs = 512 * y + xofs;
  212: 
  213: 	uint32_t tileptr = 0;
  214: 
  215: 	/*  #100: cases 0-2 index re_tlb_[abc][256] with a guest-derived
  216: 	    tile_nr = (y>>7)*16 + x/tilewidth; bound it (case 4 self-checks).  */
  217: 	if (tlb_mode <= 2 && tile_nr >= 256)
  218: 		return;
  219: 
  220: 	switch (tlb_mode) {
  221: 	case 0:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_a[tile_nr] << 16;
  222: 		break;
  223: 	case 1:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_b[tile_nr] << 16;
  224: 		break;
  225: 	case 2:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_c[tile_nr] << 16;
  226: 		break;
  227: 	case 4:	tile_nr = x >> 12;
  228: 		if (tile_nr >= 32)
  229: 			return;
  230: 		tileptr = 0x80000000 | (d->re_linear_a[tile_nr] << 12);
  231: 		ofs = x & 4095;
  232: 		// TODO... probably not correct!
  233: 		// printf("tileptr = %08x x = %i y = %i tile_nr = %i ofs = %i\n", tileptr, x, y, tile_nr, ofs);
  234: 		break;
  235: 	default:/*  sgirandompixel: this arm used to assign `*color = random()' -- with
  236: 		    its exit(1) commented out -- so a failed GET handed the CALLER a
  237: 		    random value, and draw_primitive() then wrote it to the destination
  238: 		    in the same loop iteration, since `color' is shared by the XFER GET
  239: 		    and the PUT.  DE_MODE_LIN_B (0x1400) is one of the FIVE modes
  240: 		    crmfbreg.h documents and extracts to tlb_mode 5, so this was
  241: 		    reachable with a DOCUMENTED mode, not only with a junk one.
  242: 		    MEASURED, rung 3: the destination received 0x4b1763a1, 0x2ee4b4df
  243: 		    and 0x7433cdc1 on successive runs of
  244: 		    regress/sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py.
  245: 
  246: 		    NOW DECLINED: `*color' is left alone, so the caller's own
  247: 		    `uint32_t color = fg' stands and what lands is at least
  248: 		    DETERMINISTIC.  The announce is latched because this helper is
  249: 		    per-pixel.
  250: 
  251: 		    WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, filed rather than silently omitted: the
  252: 		    caller STILL PERFORMS its dependent PUT, so a declined GET still
  253: 		    writes fg (row sgirandomskipput -- suppressing it needs a return
  254: 		    value and every GET site changed, which is more than one site);
  255: 		    LIN_B is still not implemented, and the panel refused to endorse
  256: 		    implementing it as LIN_A-with-the-other-array on symmetry alone
  257: 		    (row sgirelinb); and the COLOUR-mode switch below carries the same
  258: 		    random() shape at its own default (row sgirandomcolour).  */
  259: 		if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_BADMODE)) {
  260: 			d->warned |= SGIRE_W_BADMODE;
  261: 			/*  `dst_mode' is kept from the original text although the
  262: 			    parameter is also passed as src_mode on a GET: the probe
  263: 			    anchors on this literal, and rewording it here would be
  264: 			    scope creep AND would move an oracle anchor mid-round,
  265: 			    which is the laundering shape whatever the intent.
  266: 			    Filed as sgirenamedstmode.  */
  267: 			fatal("[ sgi_re: unimplemented dst_mode %i for "
  268: 			    "horrible_getputpixel (%s), x=%i y=%i; declined "
  269: 			    "(once) ]\n", mode, put ? "put" : "get", x, y);
  270: 		}
  271: 		return;
  272: 	}
  273: 
  274: 	// The highest bit seems to be set for a "valid" tile pointer.
  275: 	if (!(tileptr & 0x80000000)) {
  276: 		//printf("dst_mode %i, tile_nr = %i,  tileptr = 0x%llx\n", dst_mode, tile_nr, (long long)tileptr);
  277: 		//fatal("sgi gbe horrible_getputpixel: unexpected non-set high bit of tileptr?\n");
  278: 		//exit(1);
  279: 		return;
  280: 	}
  281: 	
  282: 	tileptr &= ~0x80000000;
  283: 
  284: 	uint8_t buf[8];		/* #100: bufdepth can be 8 = 1<<((mode>>8)&3) */
  285: 	if (put) {
  286: 		switch (color_mode) {
  287: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_CI:
  288: 			buf[0] = *color;
  289: 			break;
  290: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB:
  291: 			buf[0] = *color >> 24;
  292: 			buf[1] = *color >> 16;
  293: 			buf[2] = *color >> 8;
  294: 			buf[3] = 0;
  295: 			break;
  296: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA:
  297: 			buf[0] = *color >> 24;
  298: 			buf[1] = *color >> 16;
  299: 			buf[2] = *color >> 8;
  300: 			buf[3] = *color;
  301: 			break;
  302: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_ABGR:
  303: 			buf[0] = *color;
  304: 			buf[1] = *color >> 8;
  305: 			buf[2] = *color >> 16;
  306: 			buf[3] = *color >> 24;
  307: 			break;
  308: 		default:buf[0] = random();
  309: 			buf[1] = random();
  310: 			buf[2] = random();
  311: 			buf[3] = random();
  312: 			// TODO.
  313: 			fatal("[ put: color mode = 0x%x ]\n", color_mode);
  314: 		}
  315: 
  316: 		cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, tileptr + ofs,
  317: 		    buf, bufdepth, MEM_WRITE, NO_EXCEPTIONS | PHYSICAL);
  318: 	} else {
  319: 		cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, tileptr + ofs,
  320: 		    buf, bufdepth, MEM_READ, NO_EXCEPTIONS | PHYSICAL);
  321: 
  322: 		switch (color_mode) {
  323: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_CI:
  324: 			*color = buf[0];
  325: 			break;
  326: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB:
  327: 			*color = (buf[1] << 24) + (buf[2] << 16) + (buf[3] << 8);
  328: 			break;
  329: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA:
  330: 			*color = (buf[0] << 24) + (buf[1] << 16) + (buf[2] << 8) + buf[3];
  331: 			break;
  332: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_ABGR:
  333: 			*color = (buf[3] << 24) + (buf[2] << 16) + (buf[1] << 8) + buf[0];
  334: 			break;
  335: 		default:// Read "raw" 32-bit value:
  336: 			*color = (buf[3] << 24) + (buf[2] << 16) + (buf[1] << 8) + buf[0];
  337: 			fatal("[ get: color mode = 0x%x ]\n", color_mode);
  338: 		}
  339: 	}
  340: }
  341: 
  342: 
  343: /*
  344:  *  SGI "re", NetBSD sources describes it as a "rendering engine".
  345:  */
  346: 
  347: DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_re)
  348: {
  349: 	struct sgi_re_data *d = (struct sgi_re_data *) extra;
  350: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
  351: 
  352: 	idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
  353: 
  354: 	relative_addr += 0x1000;
  355: 
  640: 	}
  641: 
  642: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  643: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
  644: 
  645: 	return 1;
  646: }
  647: 
  648: 
  649: /*
  650:  *  dev_sgi_re_init():
  651:  */
  652: void dev_sgi_re_init(struct machine *machine, struct memory *mem, uint64_t baseaddr)
  653: {
  654: 	struct sgi_re_data *d;
  655: 
  656: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct sgi_re_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct sgi_re_data)));
  657: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct sgi_re_data));
  658: 
  659: 	memory_device_register(mem, "sgi_re", baseaddr + 0x1000, DEV_SGI_RE_LENGTH,
  660: 	    dev_sgi_re_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
  661: 
  662: 	dev_sgi_de_init(mem, baseaddr + 0x2000, d);
  663: 	dev_sgi_mte_init(mem, baseaddr + 0x3000, d);
  664: 	dev_sgi_de_status_init(mem, baseaddr + 0x4000, d);
  665: }
  666: 
  667: 
  668: 
  669: /****************************************************************************/
  670: 
  671: /*
  672:  *  SGI "de", NetBSD sources describes it as a "drawing engine".
  673:  */
  674: 
  675: void draw_primitive(struct cpu* cpu, struct sgi_re_data *d)
  676: {
  677: 	uint32_t op = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_PRIMITIVE - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  678: 	uint32_t drawmode = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_DRAWMODE - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  679: 	uint32_t dst_mode = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_MODE_DST - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  680: 	uint32_t src_mode = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_MODE_SRC - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  681: 	uint32_t fg = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_FG - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  682: 	uint32_t bg = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_BG - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  683: 	uint32_t rop = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_ROP - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  684: 
  685: 	uint32_t stipple_mode = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_STIPPLE_MODE - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  686: 	uint32_t pattern = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_STIPPLE_PAT - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  687: 	int nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_left = (stipple_mode >> DE_STIP_STRTIDX_SHIFT) & 31;
  688: 	int nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_right = 31 - ((stipple_mode >> DE_STIP_MAXIDX_SHIFT) & 31);
  689: 	pattern >>= nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_right;
  690: 	pattern <<= nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_right;
  691: 	pattern <<= nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_left;
  692: 
  693: 	int nr_of_bits_in_the_middle = 32 - nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_left - nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_right;
  694: 
  695: 	if (stipple_mode & 0xe0e0ffff)
  696: 		fatal("[ sgi_de: UNIMPLEMENTED stipple_mode bits: 0x%08x ]\n", stipple_mode);
  697: 
  698: 	uint32_t x1 = (d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_0 - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)] >> 16) & 0x7ff;
  699: 	uint32_t y1 = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_0 - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)]& 0x7ff;
  700: 	uint32_t x2 = (d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_1 - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)] >> 16) & 0x7ff;
  701: 	uint32_t y2 = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_1 - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)]& 0x7ff;
  702: 	size_t x, y;
  703: 
  704: 	debug("[ sgi_de: STARTING DRAWING COMMAND: op = 0x%08x,"
  705: 	    " drawmode=0x%x src_mode=0x%x dst_mode=0x%x x1=%i y1=%i"
  706: 	    " x2=%i y2=%i fg=0x%x bg=0x%x pattern=0x%08x ]\n",
  707: 	    op, drawmode, src_mode, dst_mode, x1, y1, x2, y2, fg, bg, pattern);
  708: 
  709: 	// bufdepth = 1, 2, or 4.
  710: 	// int dst_bufdepth = 1 << ((dst_mode >> 8) & 3);
  711: 	int src_bufdepth = 1 << ((src_mode >> 8) & 3);
  712: 
  713: 	int src_is_linear = 0;
  714: 	int src_x = -1, src_y = -1;
  715: 	int32_t step_x = 0;
  716: 	if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_XFER_EN) {
  717: 		uint32_t addr_src = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_ADDR_SRC - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  718: 		uint32_t strd_src = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_STRD_SRC - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  719: 		step_x = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_STEP_X - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  720: 		int32_t step_y = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_STEP_Y - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  721: 		uint32_t addr_dst = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_ADDR_DST - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  722: 		uint32_t strd_dst = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_STRD_DST - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  723: 
  724: 		src_is_linear = ((src_mode & 0x00001c00) >> 10) > 3;
  725: 
  726: 		if (src_is_linear) {
  727: 			src_x = addr_src;
  728: 			src_y = 0;
  729: 		} else {
  730: 			src_x = (addr_src >> 16) & 0x7ff;
  731: 			src_y = addr_src & 0x7ff;
  732: 		}
  733: 
  734: 		if (step_x != src_bufdepth || (step_y != 0 && step_y != 1)) {
  735: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: unimplemented XFER addr_src=0x%x src_bufdepth=%i "
  736: 				"strd_src=0x%x step_x=0x%x step_y=0x%x "
  737: 				"addr_dst=0x%x strd_dst=0x%x ]\n",
  738: 				addr_src, src_bufdepth, strd_src, step_x, step_y, addr_dst, strd_dst);
  739: 
  740: 			// exit(1);
  741: 		}
  742: 	}
  743: 
  744: 	if (!(drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_PLANEMASK)) {
  745: 		printf("!DE_DRAWMODE_PLANEMASK: TODO\n");
  958: 
  959: DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_de)
  960: {
  961: 	struct sgi_re_data *d = (struct sgi_re_data *) extra;
  962: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
  963: 	int regnr;
  964: 	int startFlag = relative_addr & CRIME_DE_START;
  965: 
  966: 	relative_addr &= ~CRIME_DE_START;
  967: 
  968: 	idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
  969: 	regnr = relative_addr / sizeof(uint32_t);
  970: 
  971: 	relative_addr += 0x2000;
  972: 
  973: 	/*
  974: 	 *  Treat all registers as read/write, by default.  Sometimes these
  975: 	 *  are accessed as 32-bit words, sometimes as 64-bit words to access
  976: 	 *  two adjacent registers in one operation.
  977: 	 */
  978: 	if (len == 8) {
  979: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
  980: 			d->de_reg[regnr] = idata >> 32ULL;
  981: 			d->de_reg[regnr+1] = idata;
  982: 		} else
  983: 			odata = ((uint64_t)d->de_reg[regnr] << 32ULL) +
  984: 			    d->de_reg[regnr+1];
  985: 	} else if (len == 4) {
  986: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
  987: 			d->de_reg[regnr] = idata;
  988: 		else
  989: 			odata = d->de_reg[regnr];
  990: 	} else {
  991: 		/*
  992: 		 *  #498: THIS USED TO END THE HOST PROCESS.  A guest choosing an
  993: 		 *  access width is not an emulator error -- one `sb' anywhere in
  994: 		 *  this 4 KB window did it.  Witnessed at rung 3 on an unmodified
  995: 		 *  -E sgi -e ip32: sb, sh, lbu and lhu each gave exactly one
  996: 		 *  "len = N not implemented" and exit(1), 4/4.
  997: 		 *
  998: 		 *  *** THE SHAPE IS NOT #497's SPLICE, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS
  999: 		 *  MEASURED, NOT STYLISTIC. ***  #497's tail only answered a read.
 1000: 		 *  THIS function's tail also calls draw_primitive() when the guest
 1001: 		 *  set CRIME_DE_START, so falling through would run a rendering
 1002: 		 *  primitive for an access we just refused.
 1003: 		 *
 1004: 		 *  Clearing startFlag and falling through -- the obvious repair --
 1005: 		 *  scores 24/25: the declined access still reaches the switch
 1006: 		 *  below, which announces e.g. "TODO: non-zero CRIME_DE_SCISSOR"
 1007: 		 *  about a write THAT NEVER HAPPENED, from an idata this arm
 1008: 		 *  discarded.  Shipping a wrong record inside a diagnostic is the
 1009: 		 *  same class the ACCEPT member of the option set was found
 1010: 		 *  through.  The goto form scores 25/25.
 1011: 		 *
 1012: 		 *  `goto <label>' to a common exit is house idiom, not an
 1013: 		 *  invention: 10 device files use goto and 8 of those use it
 1014: 		 *  inside a DEVICE_ACCESS body (dc7085, i80321, le, mk48txx,
 1015: 		 *  ne2000, pvr, vr41xx, wdc).
 1016: 		 *
 1017: 		 *  It is not an early `return' either.  Control still reaches the
 1018: 		 *  common exit, so a declined READ is answered with odata (0) via
 1019: 		 *  memory_writemax64 and the function still returns 1.  `return 0'
 1020: 		 *  would be coerced to -1 by cpus/memory_rw.c and raise
 1021: 		 *  EXCEPTION_DBE -- turning a refusal into a guest bus error.
 1022: 		 */
 1023: 		if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_DE_LEN)) {
 1024: 			d->warned |= SGIRE_W_DE_LEN;
 1025: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: unimplemented len=%i -- declined."
 1026: 			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len);
 1027: 		}
 1028: 		goto de_declined;
 1029: 	}
 1030: 
 1031: 	switch (relative_addr) {
 1032: 
 1033: 	case CRIME_DE_MODE_SRC:
 1034: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_MODE_SRC: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1035: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1036: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1037: 		break;
 1038: 
 1039: 	case CRIME_DE_MODE_DST:
 1040: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_MODE_DST: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1041: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1042: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1043: 		break;
 1044: 
 1045: 	case CRIME_DE_CLIPMODE:
 1046: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_CLIPMODE: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1047: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1048: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1049: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
 1050: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: TODO: non-zero CRIME_DE_CLIPMODE: 0x%016llx ]\n", idata);
 1051: 		break;
 1052: 
 1053: 	case CRIME_DE_DRAWMODE:
 1054: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_DRAWMODE: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1055: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1056: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1057: 		break;
 1058: 
 1059: 	case CRIME_DE_SCRMASK0:
 1060: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_SCRMASK0: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1061: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1062: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1063: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
 1064: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: TODO: non-zero CRIME_DE_SCRMASK0: 0x%016llx ]\n", idata);
 1065: 		break;
 1066: 
 1067: 	case CRIME_DE_SCRMASK1:
 1068: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_SCRMASK1: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1069: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1070: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1071: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
 1072: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: TODO: non-zero CRIME_DE_SCRMASK1: 0x%016llx ]\n", idata);
 1073: 		break;
 1074: 
 1075: 	case CRIME_DE_SCRMASK2:
 1076: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_SCRMASK2: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1077: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1078: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1079: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
 1080: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: TODO: non-zero CRIME_DE_SCRMASK2: 0x%016llx ]\n", idata);
 1081: 		break;
 1082: 
 1083: 	case CRIME_DE_SCRMASK3:
 1084: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_SCRMASK3: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1085: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1086: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1087: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
 1088: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: TODO: non-zero CRIME_DE_SCRMASK3: 0x%016llx ]\n", idata);
 1089: 		break;
 1090: 
 1091: 	case CRIME_DE_SCRMASK4:
 1092: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_SCRMASK4: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1093: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1094: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1095: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
 1096: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: TODO: non-zero CRIME_DE_SCRMASK4: 0x%016llx ]\n", idata);
 1097: 		break;
 1098: 
 1099: 	case CRIME_DE_SCISSOR:
 1100: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_SCISSOR: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1101: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1102: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1103: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
 1104: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: TODO: non-zero CRIME_DE_SCISSOR: 0x%016llx ]\n", idata);
 1105: 		break;
 1106: 
 1107: 	case CRIME_DE_SCISSOR + 4:
 1108: 		// NetBSD writes 0x3fff3fff here. "High" part of SCISSOR register?
 1109: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_SCISSOR+4: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1110: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1111: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1112: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0x3fff3fff)
 1113: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: TODO: CRIME_DE_SCISSOR+4: 0x%016llx ]\n", idata);
 1114: 		break;
 1115: 
 1116: 	case CRIME_DE_PRIMITIVE:
 1117: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_PRIMITIVE: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1118: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1119: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1120: 		break;
 1121: 
 1122: 	case CRIME_DE_WINOFFSET_SRC:
 1123: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_WINOFFSET_SRC: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1124: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1125: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1126: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
 1127: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: TODO: non-zero CRIME_DE_WINOFFSET_SRC: 0x%016llx ]\n", idata);
 1128: 		break;
 1129: 
 1130: 	case CRIME_DE_WINOFFSET_DST:
 1131: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_WINOFFSET_DST: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1132: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1133: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1134: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
 1135: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: TODO: non-zero CRIME_DE_WINOFFSET_DST: 0x%016llx ]\n", idata);
 1136: 		break;
 1137: 
 1138: 	case CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_0:
 1139: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_0: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1140: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1141: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1142: 		break;
 1143: 
 1144: 	case CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_1:
 1145: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_1: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1146: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1147: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1148: 		break;
 1149: 
 1150: 	case CRIME_DE_XFER_ADDR_SRC:
 1151: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_XFER_ADDR_SRC: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1152: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1153: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1154: 		break;
 1155: 
 1156: 	case CRIME_DE_XFER_STRD_SRC:
 1157: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_XFER_STRD_SRC: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1158: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1159: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1160: 		break;
 1161: 
 1162: 	case CRIME_DE_XFER_STEP_X:
 1163: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_XFER_STEP_X: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1164: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1165: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1166: 		break;
 1167: 
 1168: 	case CRIME_DE_XFER_STEP_Y:
 1169: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_XFER_STEP_Y: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1170: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1171: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1172: 		break;
 1173: 
 1174: 	case CRIME_DE_XFER_ADDR_DST:
 1175: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_XFER_ADDR_DST: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1176: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1177: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1178: 		break;
 1179: 
 1180: 	case CRIME_DE_XFER_STRD_DST:
 1181: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_XFER_STRD_DST: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1182: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1183: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1184: 		break;
 1185: 
 1186: 	case CRIME_DE_STIPPLE_MODE:
 1187: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_STIPPLE_MODE: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1188: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1189: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1190: 		break;
 1191: 
 1192: 	case CRIME_DE_STIPPLE_PAT:
 1193: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_STIPPLE_PAT: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1194: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1195: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1196: 		break;
 1197: 
 1198: 	case CRIME_DE_FG:
 1199: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_FG: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1200: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1201: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1202: 		break;
 1203: 
 1204: 	case CRIME_DE_BG:
 1205: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_BG: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1206: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1207: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1208: 		break;
 1209: 
 1210: 	case CRIME_DE_ROP:
 1211: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_ROP: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1212: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1213: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1214: 		break;
 1215: 
 1216: 	case CRIME_DE_PLANEMASK:
 1217: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_PLANEMASK: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1218: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1219: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1220: 		break;
 1221: 
 1222: 	case CRIME_DE_NULL:
 1223: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_NULL: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1224: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1225: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1226: 		break;
 1227: 
 1228: 	case CRIME_DE_FLUSH:
 1229: 		debug("[ sgi_de: %s CRIME_DE_FLUSH: 0x%016llx ]\n",
 1230: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? "write to" : "read from",
 1231: 		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE ? (long long)idata : (long long)odata);
 1232: 		break;
 1233: 
 1234: 	default:
 1235: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 1236: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: unimplemented write to "
 1237: 			    "address 0x%llx, data=0x%016llx ]\n",
 1238: 			    (long long)relative_addr, (long long)idata);
 1239: 		else
 1240: 			fatal("[ sgi_de: unimplemented read from address"
 1241: 			    " 0x%llx ]\n", (long long)relative_addr);
 1242: 	}
 1243: 
 1244: 	if (startFlag)
 1245: 		draw_primitive(cpu, d);
 1246: 
 1247:  de_declined:
 1248: 	/*  #498: a declined access jumps here, skipping BOTH the register
 1249: 	    switch and draw_primitive(), and is still answered.  */
 1250: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 1251: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 1252: 
 1253: 	return 1;
 1254: }
 1255: 
 1256: 
 1257: /*
 1258:  *  dev_sgi_de_init():
 1259:  */
 1260: void dev_sgi_de_init(struct memory *mem, uint64_t baseaddr, struct sgi_re_data *d)
 1261: {
 1262: 	memory_device_register(mem, "sgi_de", baseaddr, DEV_SGI_DE_LENGTH,
 1263: 	    dev_sgi_de_access, (void *)d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 1264: }
 1265: 
 1266: 
 1267: /****************************************************************************/
 1268: 
 1269: /*
 1270:  *  SGI "mte", NetBSD sources describes it as a "memory transfer engine".
 1271:  *
 1272:  *  If the relative address has the 0x0800 (CRIME_DE_START) flag set, it means
 1273:  *  "go ahead with the transfer". Otherwise, it is just reads and writes of the
 1274:  *  registers.
 1275:  */
 1276: 
 1277: void do_mte_transfer(struct cpu* cpu, struct sgi_re_data *d)
 1278: {
 1279: 	uint32_t mode = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_MODE - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 1280: 	uint32_t src0 = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_SRC0 - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 1281: 	uint32_t src1 = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_SRC1 - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 1282: 	uint32_t dst0 = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_DST0 - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 1283: 	uint32_t dst1 = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_DST1 - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 1284: 	int32_t src_y_step = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_SRC_Y_STEP - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 1285: 	int32_t dst_y_step = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_DST_Y_STEP - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 1286: 	uint32_t dstlen = dst1 - dst0 + 1, fill_addr;
 1287: 	unsigned char zerobuf[4096];
 1288: 	int depth = 8 << ((mode & MTE_MODE_DEPTH_MASK) >> MTE_DEPTH_SHIFT);
 1289: 	int src = (mode & MTE_MODE_SRC_BUF_MASK) >> MTE_SRC_TLB_SHIFT;
 1290: 	uint32_t bytemask = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_BYTEMASK - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 1291: 	uint32_t bg = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_BG - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 1292: 
 1293: 	debug("[ sgi_mte: STARTING: mode=0x%08x src0=0x%08x src1=0x%08x src_y_step=%i dst0=0x%08x,"
 1294: 	    " dst1=0x%08x dst_y_step=%i bg=0x%x bytemask=0x%x ]\n",
 1295: 	    mode,
 1296: 	    src0, src1, src_y_step,
 1297: 	    dst0, dst1, dst_y_step,
 1298: 	    bg, bytemask);
 1299: 
 1300: 	if (dst_y_step != 0 && dst_y_step != 1 && dst_y_step != -1) {
 1301: 		fatal("[ sgi_mte: TODO! unimplemented dst_y_step %i ]", dst_y_step);
 1302: 		// exit(1);
 1303: 	}
 1304: 
 1305: 	if (mode & MTE_MODE_STIPPLE) {
 1306: 		fatal("[ sgi_mte: unimplemented MTE_MODE_STIPPLE ]");
 1307: 		exit(1);
 1308: 	}
 1309: 
 1310: 	int src_tlb = (mode & MTE_MODE_SRC_BUF_MASK) >> MTE_SRC_TLB_SHIFT;
 1311: 	int dst_tlb = (mode & MTE_MODE_DST_BUF_MASK) >> MTE_DST_TLB_SHIFT;
 1312: 	
 1313: 	if (src > MTE_TLB_C) {
 1314: 		fatal("[ sgi_mte: unimplemented SRC ]");
 1315: 		exit(1);
 1316: 	}
 1317: 
 1318: 	switch (dst_tlb) {
 1319: 	case MTE_TLB_A:
 1320: 	case MTE_TLB_B:
 1321: 	case MTE_TLB_C:
 1322: 		// Used by NetBSD's crmfb_fill_rect. It puts graphical
 1323: 		// coordinates in dst0 and dst1.
 1324: 		{
 1325: 			int x1 = (dst0 >> 16) & 0xfff;
 1326: 			int y1 = dst0 & 0xfff;
 1327: 			int x2 = (dst1 >> 16) & 0xfff;
 1328: 			int y2 = dst1 & 0xfff;
 1329: 			x1 /= (depth / 8);
 1330: 			x2 /= (depth / 8);
 1331: 
 1332: 			int src_x1 = (src0 >> 16) & 0xfff;
 1333: 			int src_y1 = src0 & 0xfff;
 1334: 			// int src_x2 = (src1 >> 16) & 0xfff;
 1335: 			// int src_y2 = src1 & 0xfff;
 1336: 			src_x1 /= (depth / 8);
 1337: 			// src_x2 /= (depth / 8);
 1338: 
 1339: 			int dx = x1 > x2 ? -1 : 1;
 1340: 			int dy = y1 > y2 ? -1 : 1;
 1341: 			
 1342: 			uint32_t src_mode = (src_tlb << 10) + (((mode & MTE_MODE_DEPTH_MASK) >> MTE_DEPTH_SHIFT) << 8);
 1343: 			uint32_t dst_mode = (dst_tlb << 10) + (((mode & MTE_MODE_DEPTH_MASK) >> MTE_DEPTH_SHIFT) << 8);
 1344: 
 1345: 			// Hack. The MTE perhaps doesn't deal with colors per se,
 1346: 			// but this makes sure that we copy 32 bits when doing 32-bit
 1347: 			// transfers.
 1348: 			if (depth == 4) {
 1349: 				src_mode |= DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA;
 1350: 				dst_mode |= DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA;
 1351: 			}
 1352: 
 1353: 			int src_y = src_y1;
 1354: 			for (int y = y1; y != y2+dy; y += dy) {
 1355: 				int src_x = src_x1;
 1356: 				
 1357: 				for  (int x = x1; x != x2+dx; x += dx) {
 1358: 					if (mode & MTE_MODE_COPY) {
 1359: 						horrible_getputpixel(0, cpu, d,
 1360: 							src_x, src_y, &bg, src_mode);
 1361: 						src_x += dx;
 1362: 					}
 1363: 
 1364: 					horrible_getputpixel(1, cpu, d, x, y, &bg, dst_mode);
 1365: 				}
 1366: 				
 1367: 				src_y += dy;
 1368: 			}
 1369: 		}
 1370: 		break;
 1371: 	case MTE_TLB_LIN_A:
 1372: 	case MTE_TLB_LIN_B:
 1373: 		// Used by the PROM to zero-fill memory (?).
 1374: 		if (mode & MTE_MODE_COPY) {
 1375: 			fatal("[ sgi_mte: unimplemented MTE_MODE_COPY ]");
 1376: 			exit(1);
 1377: 		}
 1378: 
 1379: 		if (depth != 8) {
 1380: 			fatal("[ sgi_mte: unimplemented MTE_DEPTH_x ]");
 1381: 			exit(1);
 1382: 		}
 1383: 
 1384: 		debug("[ sgi_mte: LINEAR TRANSFER: mode=0x%08x dst0=0x%016llx,"
 1385: 		    " dst1=0x%016llx (length 0x%llx), dst_y_step=%i bg=0x%x, bytemask=0x%x ]\n",

 1390: 		if (bytemask != 0xffffffff) {
 1391: 			fatal("unimplemented MTE bytemask 0x%08x\n", (int)bytemask);
 1392: 			exit(1);
 1393: 		}
 1394: 
 1395: 		/*
 1396: 		 *  Horrible hack:
 1397: 		 *
 1398: 		 *  During bootup, the PROM fills memory at 0x40000000 and
 1399: 		 *  forward. This corresponds to the lowest possible RAM address.
 1400: 		 *  However, these fills are not going via the CPU's cache,
 1401: 		 *  which contains things such as the return address on the
 1402: 		 *  stack. If we _really_ write this data in the emulator (which
 1403: 		 *  doesn't emulate the cache), it would overwrite the stack.
 1404: 		 *
 1405: 		 *  So let's not.
 1406: 		 *
 1407: 		 *  (If some guest OS or firmware variant actually depends on
 1408: 		 *  the ability to write to the start of memory this way,
 1409: 		 *  it would not work in the emulator.)
 1410: 		 */
 1411: 		// if (dst0 >= 0x40000000 && dst0 < 0x40004000 && dst1 > 0x40004000) {
 1412: 		//	dst0 += 0x4000;
 1413: 		//	dstlen -= 0x4000;
 1414: 		// }
 1415: 
 1416: 		/*
 1417: 		 *  HUH?
 1418: 		 *
 1419: 		 *  Note that due to a bug (?) in the PROM firmware when it
 1420: 		 *  is setting up the TLB entries, only every _second_ page is
 1421: 		 *  actually put correctly in the TLB. This means that even
 1422: 		 *  though it then tries to fill 0x40000000 .. 0x40007fff with
 1423: 		 *  zeroes, it only fills 0x40001000 .. 0x40001fff,
 1424: 		 *  0x40003000 .. 0x40003fff and so on!
 1425: 		 *
 1426: 		 *  So the Horrible hack above is not needed.
 1427: 		 *
 1428: 		 *  Ironic.
 1429: 		 */

 1431: 		memset(zerobuf, bg, dstlen < sizeof(zerobuf) ? dstlen : sizeof(zerobuf));

 1433: 		while (dstlen != 0) {

 1435: 			if (dstlen > sizeof(zerobuf))
 1436: 				fill_len = sizeof(zerobuf);
 1437: 			else
 1438: 				fill_len = dstlen;
 1439: 
 1440: 			uint64_t starting_page = fill_addr & ~0xfff;
 1441: 			uint64_t ending_page = (fill_addr + fill_len - 1) & ~0xfff;
 1442: 			if (starting_page != ending_page) {
 1443: 				fill_len = starting_page + 4096 - fill_addr;
 1444: 			}
 1445: 
 1446: 			// Find starting_page in the TLB in question.
 1447: 			starting_page >>= 12;
 1448: 			uint32_t *tlb = dst_tlb == MTE_TLB_LIN_A ? d->re_linear_a : d->re_linear_b;
 1449: 			int match = 0;
 1450: 			for (int i = 0; i < 32; ++i) {
 1451: 				uint32_t entry = tlb[i];
 1452: 				if (entry & 0x80000000) {
 1453: 					entry &= ~0x80000000;
 1454: 					if (entry == starting_page) {
 1455: 						match = 1;
 1456: 						break;
 1457: 					}
 1458: 				}
 1459: 			}
 1460: 			
 1461: 			if (match) {
 1462: 				cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, fill_addr, zerobuf, fill_len,
 1463: 					MEM_WRITE, NO_EXCEPTIONS | PHYSICAL);
 1464: 			} else {
 1465: 				debug("[ sgi_mte: WARNING: address 0x%x not found in TLB? Ignoring fill. ]\n",
 1466: 					(long long)fill_addr);
 1467: 			}
 1468: 
 1469: 			fill_addr += fill_len;
 1470: 			dstlen -= (uint32_t)fill_len;	/* OB-23: subtract bytes actually filled */
 1471: 		}
 1472: 		break;
 1473: 	default:
 1474: 		fatal("[ sgi_mte: TODO! unimplemented dst_tlb 0x%x ]", dst_tlb);
 1475: 	}

 1495: 	 *
 1496: 	 *  NOTE: The lowest bits are internally stored in the "low" (+0)
 1497: 	 *  register, and the higher bits are stored in the "+1" word.
 1498: 	 */

  357:  *  debug() and fatal():
  358:  *
  359:  *  These are here because of all the legacy code that uses them. Basically,
  360:  *  debug() is mapped to something that is only displayed with verbosity
  361:  *  at the default level, and fatal() is used to always print stuff. With some
  362:  *  hacks to make it work reasonable with -q and when executing normally or
  363:  *  when executing single-stepping.
  364:  */
  365: void debug(const char *fmt, ...)
  366: {
  367: 	va_list argp;
  368: 	bool ss = single_step || about_to_enter_single_step;
  369: 	int v = verbose;
  370: 	if (emul_executing)
  371: 		v--;
  372: 	if (ss)
  373: 		v++;
  374: 
  375: 	if ((quiet_mode && !ss) || v < 0)
  376: 		return;
  377: 
  378: 	va_start(argp, fmt);
  379: 	va_debug(argp, fmt);
  380: 	va_end(argp);
  381: }

   41: #        window's LAST legal 8-byte slot).  MEASURED at 13/14 each for TLB_A,
   42: #        TLB_B and TLB_C on the artifact detector;
   43: #      * GROWING any window's NEAR bound, and TRANSLATING a whole window.
   44: #        *** THIS IS THE HALF THE FIRST DRAFT DID NOT HAVE, AND IT WAS
   45: #        MEASURED MISSING RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT. ***  A one-token edit --
   46: #        `>= CRIME_RE_LINEAR_A' to `>= CRIME_RE_LINEAR_A + 0x40' -- swept the
   47: #        14-row artifact detector CLEAN, because every accept row sat at its
   48: #        window's TOP slot, and a top-slot row tests the FAR bound and NEVER
   49: #        the near one.  A base-slot row has the mirrored blindness.  Both
   50: #        halves ship, per window;

   54: #        e.g. "d->re_tlb_a[252] = 0x1122" -- so it is anchored to a SPECIFIC
   55: #        CELL by a channel outside the decode under test, which is clause
   56: #        (f)'s requirement.  A "did not reach the catch-all" oracle cannot do
   57: #        this: it is satisfied by ANY arm servicing the access.
   58: #      * *** THE CATCH-ALL'S OWN STATE SPACE, ENUMERATED RATHER THAN PATCHED.
   59: #        Added by #499 pass 4 after THREE consecutive passes each found a breach
   60: #        of ONE class -- a cell of this space left empty, repaired at exactly the
   61: #        address the mutant had used, leaving the symmetric cell open for the
   62: #        next pass.  Two of the three were MEASURED CLEAN SWEEPS of a detector
   63: #        that had just come back green. ***
   64: #
   65: #        The catch-all's behaviour is a function of FIVE inputs, so they are
   66: #        listed and each is given a row that varies under a named mutant:
   67: #
   68: #          relative_addr  component 1 (0x16e0-0x16ff) and component 2 (0x1800+),
   69: #                         AND POSITION WITHIN a component.  Component 1 is a

   71: #                         changed to `- 0x10' swept 36 rows CLEAN because all
   72: #                         four component-1 rows sat at the BASE.  `cliphi rd/wr'
   73: #                         sit at its LAST 8-byte slot (0x16f8);
   74: #          writeflag      `else if (relative_addr < CRIME_RE_LINEAR_A)' on the
   75: #                         READ arm also swept 36 rows clean -- component 1 was
   76: #                         probed by two sessions and BOTH were stores.
   77: #                         `clip rd *' and `cliphi rd *' close it;

   86: #          - component 1's LAST slot at NARROW width (the len axis is pinned at
   87: #            the base of both components and at neither far edge);
